# Debt Register — Closed Rows

**Date:** 2026-08-28
**Purpose:** Archive of retired rows from `debt_register.md`. A row lands here once its ID
and description are struck, i.e. no design work remains on it.
**Module:** cross-cutting

Read `debt_register.md` first — it carries the rules for reading a row, and the live set.
Two of those rules matter most here:

- **The status column is never struck.** It is where the closure evidence lives. Read it, not
  the strike.
- **Struck is not verified.** Some rows below are code-in and awaiting a build, or fixed and
  awaiting a single run. The strike records that the thinking is finished, not that the tree
  was exercised. Where a gate survives, the status column names it.

IDs are stable and are never reused. Rows are kept in their original register order, which is
roughly chronological and not strictly numeric.

| ~~DR-110~~ | ~~mesh: `Basis::add_source` has no allocation-capacity guard~~ | P3 | **CLOSED BY RULING 2026-08-30 (Christian).** This is accepted caller-discipline behavior: every caller must allocate exactly the number of sources it later appends. `Basis` deliberately carries no capacity member, avoiding per-entity memory growth for an invariant all current callers satisfy. The public contract is documented on `allocate_source_container()` / `add_source()` in `cl_Mesh_Basis.hpp`, and the implementation states the same constraint at `cl_Mesh_Basis.cpp`. No runtime reproducer is owed; a future caller that cannot prove the exact append count must introduce an owning container or its own capacity tracking rather than changing this contract implicitly. | n/a — accepted invariant | no |

| ~~DR-13~~ | ~~mesh: facet-capacity truncation in `Vertex::allocate_facet_container`~~ | P2 | **downgraded (2026-08-09)** — `mFacetCounter`/`mFacetCapacity` are now `uint16_t` (`cl_Vertex.hpp:62-63`) with an overflow assert on the counter (`:270`), so the wrap moved 256 → 65536. **Residual:** `allocate_facet_container` still assigns a `uint tCount` into the `uint16_t` capacity unguarded (`cl_Vertex.cpp:213-226`; the body ends at 226, not 225) — silent mod-65536 truncation remains possible in principle. **2026-08-13 sweep — confirmed, with two corrections and a caller audit that bounds it.** The narrowing is real: `uint tCount` at `:218` goes into `mFacetCapacity` at `:224` with no clamp or cast, the only guard being `BELFEM_ASSERT( mFacets == nullptr )` at `:216`. **Correction:** the overflow assert is at **`cl_Vertex.hpp:270`** (inline `increment_facet_counter`), not `cl_Vertex.cpp:270`. **No reachable overflow today:** every call site passes either the default 0 (falls back to the already-asserted counter) or a size itself derived from a `uint16_t` counter — `cl_Mesh_ConnectivityCalculator.cpp:293,652,692,715,717`, `cl_CurveFactory.cpp:290`, `cl_ProtoMesh.cpp:1234`, `cl_ThinShellFactory.cpp:2810,2818`. The single theoretical route is the Facet overload at `cl_Mesh_ConnectivityCalculator.cpp:400`/`:517`, sized from a bitset over facet indices. In debug a truncated capacity would trip `add_facet`'s assert (`cl_Vertex.cpp:233`); in release it is a heap overflow. **FIXED 2026-08-23 (Christian: "if the fix is trivial, then fix it"), plan+audit round, verified compiling.** Landed exactly as diagnosed: `BELFEM_ASSERT( tCount <= 65535, ... )` before the malloc, explicit `( uint16_t )` cast on the store into `mFacetCapacity` — mirrors the sibling `mFacetCounter` guard's idiom at `cl_Vertex.hpp:270`. Both Codex and Grok audited blind, both: safe to land as written, no change to the plan. Grok's sharpened nuance kept for the record: `:400`/`:517` are only *practically* bounded ( a pathological non-manifold mesh could still feed `tCount > 65535` through either ) — that is the reason the assert belongs, not a reason to weaken it. `make check-fast` gate run same session. Pinned: the constructor's BDF1 default, method round-trip over BDF1-5, the **startup ramp holding step one at order 1 for every BDF2-5 run** (load-bearing — `mH` is all zeros before the first shift, so a broken ramp runs into `compute_bdf_coefficients()`'s own "Invalid BDF history step size" assert), the non-BDF gate protecting the eigenvalue `MassOnly`/`StiffnessOnly` switch from stale order state, and the CN/Galerkin hard rejection — never pinned before, despite being why that clause of this row was struck rather than fixed. **Scope correction made during the work:** the variable-step coefficient formulas are **not** reachable, contrary to the first reading — `mAlpha`, `mBeta`, `mH` and `mStepCount` are private with no accessor, and `mH`/`mStepCount` are written only by `shift_fields`/`reset_fields`/`restore_savepoint`, all of which dereference `mField` and call `DofManager::initialize()`. All pass `AbortOnError = false` and assert on `info`, so a rejected `lwork` is a red test, not a process abort — and that is what makes the minimum-length test load-bearing: LAPACK, not the test, is the authority on whether the formula is understated. **spline** (`tests/math/test_Spline.cpp`): §3.4 was *using* `eval( x, 0 )` as its reference while nothing tested it. | ~~DR-45~~ | ~~mumps: two separate things, and only one was a bug. **BOTH CLAUSES NOW IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-11) — the row stays open only on its run gate.** There are **three** `INFO` sites, not the two this row asserted for a week: `MUMPS::free`, and *both* `MUMPS::solve` overloads. **(a) Worker-only warnings were never printed — FIXED 2026-08-11 across all three.** Both `INFO` reads (`MUMPS::free`, `MUMPS::solve`) gated the warning branch on `rank() == 0`, while the wrapper's own comment records that the shim exports the **rank-LOCAL** `INFO` array. Errors are fine — MUMPS propagates them, non-failing ranks see `INFO(1) = -1`, and that path was already un-gated — but warnings are not propagated, so any warning raised on a worker was silently discarded. Each rank now reports its own, tagged with the proc that raised it. Reporting only; no control flow changed. **Residual, found by Grok in the DR-45(b) jury round and confirmed by direct source read in the 2026-08-11 currentness sweep:** `5a2ddf81` fixed `MUMPS::free` and the *Vector*-RHS `MUMPS::solve`, but the **Matrix-RHS overload** still gated its warning branch on `this->rank() == 0`. (Line anchors deliberately dropped: they read `:238`/`:408`/`:563` against `5a2ddf81` and went stale the moment `78ea534d` rewrote the region. Current anchors — `MUMPS::solve` at `:243` and `:401`, `MUMPS::check_warnings` at `:1225`, and **no `rank() == 0` left on any warning path** — are given here as navigation, not as truth.) A worker-raised warning is still discarded on that path — including the `INFO(1) = +1` that clause (b) is about. **That residual is now closed too:** both `solve` overloads route their positive-`INFO(1)` branch through a new private `MUMPS::check_warnings()`, so all three sites report per rank with the proc tagged. `free()` deliberately keeps its own report-only loop — it is teardown, reached from `~MUMPS()` after `JOB = -2`, where a hard error would abort during cleanup and mask whatever caused the teardown. **(b) Should `INFO(1) = +1` be escalated to a hard error? — YES, IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-11 after a jury round (`tmp/ai_exchange/review_mumps_outofrange.md`).** `+1` means MUMPS found indices in IRN/JCN outside the matrix and **dropped those entries**, so the factorisation is of a different matrix than the one assembled — a wrong-answer class, not a numerical condition, and there is no legitimate reason for BELFEM to hand MUMPS an out-of-range index. The decoder already reports the faulty-entry count from `INFO(2)`. Argument for escalating: a silently-dropped entry cannot be noticed downstream. Argument against, and the reason it was not just done: adding an always-active abort to a solver path is exactly the change that broke every parallel run earlier the same day — the `set_currents` length guard, reverted in `61bbfa11` — so it wanted a deliberate decision rather than a tidy-up. **That incident is often mislabelled "DR-24" in this register and in the sweep prose; it is not.** DR-24 is the periodic free-cut `mPrescribedCurrents` row, closed by Christian on 2026-08-09 by deleting the dead API. The guard was a failed *attempt* at the one-time assert DR-24 had asked for, and it has no row of its own — cite `61bbfa11`, not DR-24. **Codex and Grok audited it blind and independently returned the same verdict** — escalate, and neither could construct a benign producer of `+1`. The decisive evidence is structural rather than the absence of a grep hit: fixed dofs are not discarded through a sentinel index, they live in a separate matrix (`mDirichletMatrix` is free×fixed, `mSystemMatrix` free×free, `cl_FEM_DofMgr_SolverData.cpp:350-405`), so there is no discard idiom for the escalation to break; and with `ICNTL(5) = 0` / `ICNTL(18) = 0` and `irn`/`jcn`/`A` bound only on the master (`mumpstools.f90:321,340-348`), `+1` is **host-local by construction**. **Scope guard, flagged independently by both auditors and the real hazard here:** the check tests the **bit** (`mInfo( 0 ) & 1`), never `mInfo( 0 ) > 0` — MUMPS sums the warning flags, and `+8` (iterative refinement did not converge) is *expected* on ill-conditioned HTS systems, so aborting on any positive `INFO(1)` would reproduce the DR-24 failure exactly. `BELFEM_ERROR`, not `BELFEM_ASSERT`: the latter compiles out under `NDEBUG`, leaving the release build quietly solving the wrong matrix. `+1` is never routed through `soft_fail()` — a retry re-factorises the same wrong matrix. **One pre-registered premise was refuted:** `InfoLevel::Minimal` does *not* hide the message (`Minimal = 1`, `Default = 2`, gate is `aInfoLevel <= mInfoLevel`), so an absorbed `+1` would have been printing on rank 0 all along and read by nobody — visible but unread, not silent. **One auditor disagreement, settled by source:** Codex wanted an `MPI_Allreduce` rank-uniform verdict citing STRUMPACK; that precedent is the *soft-fail* path, which returns into further collectives and must stay uniform, whereas a hard error terminates via `MPI_Abort` and needs no reduction~~ | P2 | **STRUCK 2026-08-11 on Christian's explicit instruction** — a deliberate exception to the "fixed-but-still-gating rows stay live" rule, on the same grounds as DR-42/DR-49 earlier the same day: the *design* work is finished and the residual is a single run, not a decision, a design question, or an unwritten test. **Struck ≠ verified applies here with full force** — the gate below has never been run and is unchanged by the strike; | ~~DR-59~~ | materials: ~~**latent corruption in the parallel rho gather — unreachable today, a landmine tomorrow.**~~ **FULLY CLOSED: half deleted, half was never a defect.** In `populate_rho_database_parallel`, the loop that copies received values from worker ranks declares `index_t tCount = 0 ;` and then does `tMyRho( tIndex ) = tRho( tCount ) ;` **without ever incrementing `tCount`** (`cl_Material_Metal.cpp:785-790`; identical in `cl_Material_Alloy.cpp:568-573`), so every received node would take `tRho(0)`. A second latent mismatch sits beside it: `populate_rho_database` packs owned nodes densely as `aRho(tCount++)` while the `Database` projector reads `F(node->index())` — correct only while one rank owns everything. Both are currently dead code because the METIS abort (DR-57) fires first and, even after that is cleared, `aSetProcOwners=false` leaves workers with no entities. **Anyone who later enables real distributed rho construction must fix the gather and the packing first.** Found by the Codex+Grok audit of `todo/closed/example_deck_and_material_db_repair.md`, verified by Claude at the cited lines. **RESOLVED for the gather (2026-08-10): the bodies are DELETED** — 222 lines out of `Metal` and `Alloy` (definitions + declarations) on Christian's approval, after a three-AI jury round confirmed zero callers, no virtual/base-pointer path (both are `private` non-virtual; `Alloy` is not a `Metal`), and no orphaned helpers (`create_database_mesh` and the two-arg `populate_rho_database` stay reachable from the serial builders). Thread: `tmp/ai_exchange/review_dead_code_deletion.md`. **Citation correction (Codex + Grok, independently, verified):** this row previously cited the Alloy stuck counter at `cl_Material_Alloy.cpp:568-573` — wrong, those lines were the `partition` call; the loop was at `:598-606`. Metal's `:828-833` was right. ~~**WHAT SURVIVES THE DELETION and keeps this row open:** the two-arg `populate_rho_database( Mesh*, Vector<real>& )` packs owned nodes densely (`aRho( tCount++ )`) while the projector reads them back by position (`cl_DatabaseProjector.cpp:253-257`) — a live trap for anyone enabling real distribution.~~ **NOT A DEFECT — design, ruled by Christian 2026-08-10, and the code agrees.** The rho database is *deliberately* built on the master: every solver the projector can use (MUMPS, PARDISO, SUPERLU, UMFPACK) assembles the matrix on rank 0, the system is comparatively small, and distributing it would cost more overhead than it saves — while all ranks still need the finished material. That is why `Database`'s ctor calls the Projector **collectively on every rank** (`cl_Database.cpp:29-33`) with `share`/`receive` on the non-projecting branch. Verified: the two "different" index spaces are the **same** one. `tRho` is `tMesh->create_field( "rho" )`, i.e. full node-length, and `Mesh` assigns `tNode->set_index( tCount++ )` walking that same container (`cl_Mesh.cpp:432`) — so with rank 0 owning every node, dense `aRho( tCount++ )` writes slot k for the node whose `index()` is k, exactly what `F( node->index() )` reads back. Agreement is structural, not accidental. **Residual, cosmetic only:** the `if ( tNode->owner() != tRank ) continue ;` filter is the one line implying a distribution that by design never happens — it is a permanent no-op, and the only real risk is a future reader mistaking it for evidence that partial ownership is supported | P2 | **closed (2026-08-10)** — the jury (and I) mislabelled a design decision as a latent defect; corrected by Christian | n/a | no |

| ~~DR-63~~ | ~~docs: **22 of the 23 `src/*/doc/README.md` carry no external reference or DOI**, against a documented convention saying they should; only `src/homology/doc/README.md` has one. `CLAUDE.md` asserted the convention as fact until 2026-08-11 and now hedges it. | ~~DR-66~~ | ~~linalg/gesvd: **the caller-provided work-buffer reuse path is unreachable for a buffer sized by the wrapper's own query, under MKL.** `fn_gesvd.hpp` accepts the caller's `Work` only when `Work.length() >= tRealsPerT * lwork + tRWorkSize`, where `lwork` is the **reference-LAPACK** minimum `max( 3*mn + max(m,n), 5*mn )`. MKL's `dgesvd` workspace query returns **7** for a 4×3 `'A','A'` problem where that formula gives **15** — measured, not inferred — and MKL then completes with `lwork = 7` and `info = 0`. So the buffer the wrapper grows on its first call is *smaller than its own acceptance threshold*: every subsequent call re-enters the query branch, issues a second LAPACK call, and calls `Vector::set_size` **twice** (once down to `tRealsPerT + tRWorkSize`, once back up). Worse for a caller looping over shapes — a 4×3 then a 3×2 shrinks the shared buffer 7 → 5, so "reuse one buffer across a loop", the entire reason the overload exists, silently never happens. **Not a correctness fault:** results are right and `info` is 0 throughout, which is why the suite caught it only as a length assertion. Found while repairing the two `GesvdWorkBufferReuse` failures (2026-08-11 currentness sweep); the repaired test asserts results rather than lengths and a new `GesvdWorkBufferReuseAtReferenceMinimum` pins the branch that *is* reachable, so the suite is green **without** the defect being asserted away. Fix is a design call, not a typo: either trust the query result as the acceptance floor, or keep the conservative reference floor and document that query-sized buffers are not reusable. **RULED AND FIXED (2026-08-13, Christian's go-ahead):** neither option as posed — "trust the query as the acceptance floor" is incoherent (the acceptance test must decide *without* a LAPACK call, so the floor can only be a formula), and documentation alone leaves the reuse path dead under MKL. The fix restores the invariant *a wrapper-sized buffer passes the wrapper's own acceptance test*: `lwork = std::max( lwork, work_size( Work(0) ) )` after the query (the formula value floors the vendor optimum; oversized lwork is always legal LAPACK and generally faster), plus a grow-only guard on the pre-query resize (no shrink-then-grow `set_size` pair). Backend-conditional handling was considered and deliberately NOT used — one agnostic path serves all backends, and this ruling (formula = acceptance floor, sizing = max(query, formula), buffers only grow) is the pattern for every future wrapper in the unified LAPACK interface. Tests tightened: `GesvdWorkBufferReuse` now asserts the buffer reaches the floor and holds its length across same-shape AND smaller-shape calls (backend-invariant only *because* of the fix); the `AtReferenceMinimum` note rewritten — it now pins the exact-minimum acceptance edge. **Probe-verified on the MKL+Blaze tree (2026-08-13):** standalone probe linking the prebuilt `.a`s re-measured the raw query — lwork=7 vs reference 15 for 4×3 `'A','A'` double, independently confirming the sweep's number — and with the fix one buffer runs 4×3, 4×3, 3×2 at length 15/15/15, info=0, reconstruction error ≤3.4e-15. `test_lapack.cpp` compiles clean under `-Werror` with the tree's flags but has NOT executed~~ | P2 | **fixed 2026-08-13, uncommitted** — probe-verified (double flavor); suite gate not run. **Struck 2026-08-13 on Christian's instruction; the `make check-fast` gate survives here, shared with DR-42/49** | `tests/linalg/test_lapack.cpp` `GesvdWorkBufferReuse*` under `make check-fast` with `USE_TEST=ON` — the same never-yet-run gate as DR-42/49 | no |

An in-kernel `#CHECK` probe proved the Joule path is healthy and that dissipation below the knee is ~1e-37 Ω·m × J² ≈ 0, so **the cooling cannot be source-related** — an adiabatic problem with a non-negative source cannot go below its initial temperature at all. A three-AI round (`tmp/ai_exchange/thermal_joule_missing.md`) refuted every pre-registered candidate: no default BC injected, savepoint-take/restore pairing consistent (`cl_IWG_Timestep.cpp:537-598`, `cl_FEM_Controller.cpp:2064-2073`), BDF adds `+Δt·f` not `-` (`cl_IWG_Timestep.cpp:880`), Anderson and relaxation cannot invent a colder state from a flat field, `density correction` not active in that deck. **Known: it was inherited, not freshly produced** — Codex read `memdump.hdf5` and found `/fields/T` ∈ [76.9805, 77.0001] already inside it at t = 5.23, and `hphiTrun` warm-restarts from that dump by default (opt-out, `cl_FEM_Controller.cpp:3689-3703`). Prime suspect is the pre-instrumentation era of the same campaign, when diverged thermal KSP solves were accepted silently (the DR-08 defect, fixed 2026-08-12) **This is very likely a recurrence — or the same defect — as DR-06** ("thermal-coupled: -1.9 K dip not proven fixed", open since 2026-07-09, suspects `dV(k) < 0` on wafer elements and lookup extrapolation). Same signature class: a coupled thermal run going BELOW its initial temperature with no sink available. DR-06's negative-Jacobian suspect is directly testable here — `Calculator::dV` carries a `BELFEM_ERROR( adV >= 0.0, "Negative Jacobian determinant" )` under debug only (`cl_FEM_Calculator.hpp:1969-1976`), so a release run with an inverted layer element would integrate a NEGATIVE mass and volume silently, and a negative-mass element cooling while its neighbors hold is exactly the observed band. **Treat the two rows as one investigation.** **The SPREAD is as diagnostic as the offset and was under-read at first:** the old field spanned 76.9798-76.9952, a 15 mK *range*, not a uniform 20 mK shift. With zero source and pure conduction from a uniform initial condition the field cannot develop structure at all — so the sink is **spatially localized**, which is much more consistent with DR-06's negative-`dV` suspect (a few inverted layer elements integrating negative mass, cooling locally and conducting the deficit into their neighbors) than with any global integrator drift.~~ | P1 | **MECHANISM IDENTIFIED AND QUANTIFIED 2026-08-13 — the leak is LIVE in the current binary, not inherited.** The fresh adiabatic run drifted to min T = 76.99991423348413 K by t = 50 ms (23 steps): **8.577e-5 K total, 3.73e-6 K per step, i.e. 4.8e-8 relative per step against a PETSc rtol of 1e-8** (`mRelativeTolerance = 1e-8`, `cl_SolverParameters.hpp:100`, passed to `KSPSetTolerances` at `cl_SolverPETSC.cpp:434`; atol/divtol/maxits stay `PETSC_DEFAULT`). Cause: **the thermal system is solved for ABSOLUTE temperature, so the RHS is dominated by `M·T_hist/Δt` — proportional to 77 K — and a RELATIVE Krylov tolerance therefore accepts an absolute error of ~rtol × T per step.** In a forced problem this is invisible under the real ΔT; in an adiabatic sourceless one nothing pulls it back, and because the RHS direction repeats every step the Krylov error repeats in the same direction, so it accumulates LINEARLY in step count (not as a √n random walk). Ratio to rtol is 4.8, consistent with ordinary condition-number amplification of the residual bound. **This also explains the spatial structure without invoking bad elements:** the ASM preconditioner's error pattern follows subdomain boundaries, which predicts the deficit map should mirror the MPI partition — a cheap ParaView check that would confirm the mechanism outright. Note this **weakens the DR-06 link** noted below: the tolerance mechanism explains a mK-scale drift cleanly but does not by itself reach DR-06's -1.9 K without far more steps, a looser tolerance, or a higher operating temperature ~~**MECHANISM VERIFIED BY EXECUTION 2026-08-13**~~ **RETRACTED SAME DAY — see the retraction below.** Rerun with the linear `relative tolerance` moved 1e-8 -> 1e-10 shifted the thermal nonlinear residual floor from -75.9 dB to **-95.6 dB** — a 19.7 dB move against the 20.0 dB a 100x tolerance change predicts. In absolute terms the floor went 2.57e-8 -> 2.75e-10, i.e. it sits at **2.6-2.8 x rtol both before and after**: the nonlinear residual floor IS the linear solver's tolerance, scaling 1:1 with it. The same log shows the magnetic side (STRUMPACK, direct) at **-156.5 dB = 2.2e-16**, machine precision — which is precisely why Messe et al. 2023 could specify a nonlinear 1e-11 and why no iterative solver can meet it at default settings. Predicted residual drift under the new setting: 3.7e-8 K/step, ~0.9 uK at t = 50 ms, a few tenths of a mK across a full run — comfortably below the physical signal of several mK. ~~**RETRACTION: the linear-tolerance mechanism is REFUTED**~~ — **the retraction itself was WRONG and is withdrawn; it rested on a stale viewer reading. Original retraction text kept below for the audit trail:** Rerunning at rtol 1e-10 produced min T = 76.99991423348413 at t = 50 ms — **bit-for-bit identical to the rtol 1e-8 run, all 16 digits**, on a freshly written output file. A 100x tolerance change cannot leave a solver-produced number bit-identical, so the dip does not originate in the linear solve. What was actually verified is only that the *nonlinear residual floor* tracks rtol 1:1 (-75.9 -> -95.6 dB); that floor was then wrongly assumed to set the temperature error. The warning sign was present from the start and was waved away: even at rtol 1e-8 the drift was 3.7e-6 K/step against `rtol x T` = 7.7e-7, five times too large. **The corrected reading, and it is a much sharper signature: `max = 77.0 exactly` with `min = 76.99991`.** The bulk of the field is untouched — this is a LOCALIZED, deterministic, bit-reproducible cold spot, not a global drift. The old campaign's whole-field depression (max 76.9952) is what that spot becomes after thousands of steps of conduction. **This points hard at DR-06** (inverted-Jacobian elements integrating negative mass would be exactly this: a fixed sink at a fixed place, indifferent to solver settings, identical run to run) **RE-CONFIRMED BY DIRECT FILE READ (2026-08-13, final).** The bit-identical 50 ms value was the PREVIOUS run's output still held open in the viewer (same filename `hphi_results.e-s.00001`), which is exactly why it matched to 16 digits. Reading the Exodus frames directly with `scipy.io.netcdf_file` gives, for the rtol 1e-10 run: deficit **2.60e-7 K at t = 0.05 s** against the old run's 8.577e-5 K — a **330x improvement** (100x from rtol, ~3x more from the nonlinear tolerance moving 1e-6 -> 1e-7). Growth across frames is smooth and roughly linear in step count: 2.60e-7 (0.05 s), 4.71e-7 (0.1), 6.48e-7 (0.15), 8.14e-7 (0.2), 9.35e-7 (0.3), 1.24e-6 (0.4). Extrapolated to t = 5.2 s that is ~1.6e-5 K, three orders below the physical signal — **the deck-level mitigation is sufficient in practice.** The 'localized cold spot / DR-06' reading was an artifact of the same stale frame and is withdrawn: `#nodes below 77` is 374310 at t = 0.05 s, i.e. the drift is global, as originally described. **Methodological note worth keeping: two numbers disagreed and the file was not read directly for a full exchange — a claim was verified, then retracted, then re-confirmed, all on reported values rather than measurements.** **CLOSED 2026-08-13 — defect resolved and VERIFIED BY EXECUTION TWICE.** (1) Fixed-point comparison: 8.577e-5 K of drift at t = 50 ms under rtol 1e-8 versus **2.60e-7 K at rtol 1e-10**, read directly from the Exodus frames — a 330x reduction (100x from rtol, ~3x from the nonlinear tolerance moving 1e-6 -> 1e-7). (2) Full-run behaviour, which is the stronger result and corrects the model: **the drift is SELF-LIMITING, not cumulative.** Measured across the live quench run it peaked at 3.30e-6 K near t = 1.9 s and has fallen monotonically since (9.2e-7 at t = 4.3 s, 5.6e-7 at t = 5.7 s) as conduction and real Joule heating erase it. The linear-accumulation estimate that motivated the P1 severity was pessimistic: it holds only for a field with no conduction path and no source. At the old default the same run would sit near 3.4e-3 K. **The default is now 1e-10 in source** (`cl_SolverParameters.hpp`, input contract updated in both artifacts), so no deck can inherit the problem silently. The architectural remedy is tracked separately as DR-72, non-blocking | **Deck-level mitigation VERIFIED SUFFICIENT by direct file read (2026-08-13): `relative tolerance : 1e-10` + nonlinear thermal 1e-7 gives 330x less drift, ~1.6e-5 K extrapolated over a full run.** Row stays OPEN only for the architectural fix: solve the thermal system in INCREMENT form (ΔT) so a relative tolerance is measured against the change rather than the absolute 77 K / 300 K offset, making the criterion scale-free and removing the failure mode rather than shrinking it. **DR-06 link downgraded, not closed:** the localized-cold-spot signature that motivated it came from the stale frame; the drift is global. A debug run confirming no layer element has det(J) <= 0 is still worth doing on its own merits (the `adV >= 0.0` guard is debug-only, `cl_FEM_Calculator.hpp:1969-1976`), but it is no longer this row's blocking question. Parser hazard below stands unchanged. | ~~**YES**~~ resolved — default fixed in source, drift self-limiting |

| ~~DR-80~~ | ~~sparse: does SuperLU mutate the input CSR/CSC index arrays in place?~~ **REFUTED 2026-08-16 by reading the SuperLU sources** (upstream `xiaoyeli/superlu` master; the local `tmp/superlu` turned out to be a saved GitHub landing PAGE, not the tree, so the three routines were read from the upstream raw sources). All three entry points the wrapper uses are read-only on the input arrays: `get_perm_c()` writes only `perm_c` and builds A'A / A'+A in its OWN `SUPERLU_MALLOC`'d arrays (`getata`/`at_plus_a`); `sp_preorder()` never writes `Astore->{nzval,rowind,colptr}` and produces AC (SLU_NCP) by ALIASING `Astore->nzval` and `Astore->rowind` while allocating only its own `colbeg`/`colend`; `dgstrf()` takes `Astore->{nzval,rowind,colbeg,colend}` into local read pointers and only scatters (`dense[asub[k]] = a[k]`) — no writes, and no equilibration inside dgstrf (that lives in the dgssvx driver, which BELFEM does not call). Christian's prior was correct. Two facts worth keeping: (1) AC aliases the SpMatrix VALUE array, so a `SamePattern` refactorization picks up re-assembled values automatically — intended and safe; (2) `SUPERLU::solve` re-runs `symbolic()` whenever `&aMatrix != mMatrix` (`cl_SolverSUPERLU.cpp:48-51, 61-64`), so alternating system/Jacobian solves re-wrap the arrays rather than reusing a stale alias | P2 | closed (2026-08-16) — no defect; linked pairs are safe under SuperLU | source read of get_perm_c.c / sp_preorder.c / dgstrf.c | no |

| ~~DR-108~~ | ~~mesh/homology: **the hanging-node source counter is a `uint8_t`, so any cut duplicate touched by 255 or more cuts silently loses most of its constraint — on the gantry deck ( 464 cuts ) this pins 302 duplicate nodes to a constant potential, severs the cut, and leaves the upper yoke arm at |B| = 0.** Opened 2026-08-26 as a ferromagnetic-cut formulation question ( "iron-piercing cuts leave a flux-starved island" ); **re-rooted the same day by measurement — the defect is arithmetic and material-blind.** `Basis::mNumberOfSources` is `uint8_t` ( `cl_Mesh_Basis.hpp:33` ). `CutSet::create_duplicates` ( `cl_CutSet.cpp:44-76` ) builds each cut duplicate's source list as [ N abstract current nodes, then the ORIGINAL node ], length N+1, all weights 1.0. `Basis::set_sources` ( `cl_Mesh_Basis.cpp:314-345`, plus the sibling overloads at `:192` and `:232` ) does `mNumberOfSources = aSources.size()` — silent `size_t -> uint8_t` narrowing — then `malloc`s AND copies the wrapped count, so nothing is smashed and nothing aborts. At N = 464, 465 wraps to 209: the 209 surviving sources are all abstract current nodes and **the original node, last in the list, is dropped**, so `phi_dup = phi_org + sum I_c` degenerates to `phi_dup = 209*I` and the cut stops transmitting flux. **Measured proof ( serial gantry, `hphi_results.e-s.00012`, all 464 tape currents exactly 42.0112 A ): 32 distinct N values from 425 to 464, every group pinned to exactly `((N+1) mod 256) * I`, min == max within each group; the 183 nodes at N = 464 all read phi = 8780.341 = 209 x 42.0112 regardless of position.** Below the cliff the machinery is exact: 1140/1140 non-overflow cut pairs give `phi_dup - phi_org = +N*I` with one consistent sign, and B is continuous across them ( iron0/iron1, 196 pairs at N = 232, max |dB| = 0.0000 T ) while it is not across the overflowing trunk ( 2.2081 T ). Three claims from the opening brief are **refuted**: the "inconsistent per-fragment MMFs" ( 8818/8780/10250 ) are not MMFs but the wrapped constant and differences against it; the "162 pos / 141 neg mixed jump signs" were an unordered-pair artifact ( all 303 iron pairs are sign-coherent, so this is NOT another probe-4b instance ); and the defect is not ferromagnetic — 205 of the 302 corrupted nodes are AIR nodes and the trunk starts on the outer air boundary, so Christian's ferro-cut mitigation flag would have hidden the iron island and left the air trunk broken. Contributing condition, already registered as DR-71: `mSuggestHomologies` is hardcoded `true` ( `cl_CutFactory.hpp:91`, no setter in the tree ), so `reduce_complexPellikkaGeneralized()` never runs and generator supports are never minimized — which is why all 464 generators pile onto one trunk in the first place. Prior art: `cl_Vertex.hpp:59-61` promoted `mFacetCounter` to `uint16_t` with the comment "this one needs to be larger due to cohomoligies" — the same fix, applied to the wrong counter. Untested and falsifiable: at exactly N = 255 the count wraps to **0**, `is_hanging()` returns false, and the duplicate becomes a free unconstrained dof, which is worse than pinning; this deck has no CutSet with N in 233..424 so the cliff position is read off the code, not measured — **do not describe the safe range as "N <= 232"**. Brief with the fix shape ( D1-D3, gates G1-G3, residuals O1-O2 ) and a scipy-only reproducer: `todo/closed/ferro_cut_flux_island.md`; round record: `devlog/dl20260826_dr108_uint8_source_overflow.md`~~ | P1 | **FIXED and VERIFIED 2026-08-26 by Christian ( commit `d0f4db7d`, "mesh: widen the hanging-source counter so cut trunks keep their constraint" ).** `Basis::mNumberOfSources` is now `uint16_t` with `<limits>`, and `BELFEM_ERROR` width guards sit in all three `set_sources` overloads, `allocate_source_container`, `add_source`, `increment_dof_counter` and `insert_dof` ( `mNumberOfDofs` deliberately stays `uint8_t`, guarded ). **The "but it saves memory" premise was measured and is false:** `sizeof` is byte-identical at uint8 / uint16 / uint32 / uint64 for `Basis` ( 80 ), `Node` ( 184 ), `Edge` ( 144 ), `Face` ( 176 ) and `Element` ( 128 ) — each counter is followed by a pointer and sat in alignment padding, so the byte was never saved. **Gate G1 ran** ( serial gantry, `gantry.bfm` regenerated at 47.8 MB against the poisoned 46.7 MB, `hphi_results.e-s.00016`, t = 1.6 s, I = 55.8992 A ) and every number moved the right way: **0 nodes with the wrap signature ( was 302 )**; **1371 / 1371 cut pairs at exactly `phi_dup - phi_org = +N*I`, 0 mismatches** ( was 1140 right / 231 wrong ); **1366 / 1366 same-kind cut pairs with B continuous to < 1e-6 T** ( was 5 broken, up to 1.14 T ); **0 dead islands anywhere in the mesh** ( was the 375-node iron arm, an 8-node fragment and two air islands ); the dead upper yoke arm now carries |B| mean 0.4618 / max 0.6117 T with varying phi ( was 0.0000 / 0.0003 at phi = const ); no NaN/Inf, |B| max 1.83 T. | ~~DR-30~~ | ~~postproc: aura stale-T in `compute_superconductor*` near partition boundaries (thermal-coupled JJc diagnostic)~~ | P2 | **REWRITTEN 2026-08-13 sweep — the row as written does NOT hold; a narrower defect survives.** The generic "stale T on aura" reading is refuted: T on ghost nodes is written by every thermal solve (`DofManager::solve()` → `mFieldData->distribute( mIWG->all_fields() )`, `cl_FEM_DofManager.cpp:1032-1038`, invoked at `cl_FEM_Controller.cpp:1359`/`:1863`), and the comm table's node set covers the aura layer (`cl_Mesh_Distributor.cpp:306-317,353-359`); Anderson writes fields *before* that distribute, so it does not reopen the hole. **What actually survives:** (1) the postprocess path distributes only `mPostprocessorSourceFields` = `{edge_h, face_h}`/`{phi}` (`cl_FEM_DofManager.cpp:1292`) — T is never in that list, so correctness rests entirely on a preceding thermal solve+distribute, and any future postprocess-without-solve path reopens this; (2) a real stale-**element** path at `cl_FEM_Calculator.cpp:3003-3012` — if `tPeer->group()->element_exists()` is false the thermal peer silently keeps its PREVIOUS element, so `q()` returns another element's T, and `compute_superconductor` re-checks only `block_exists` (`cl_MaxwellPostprocessor.cpp:674`,`:747`), never `element_exists`, with no assert. Severity drops from "wrong on every boundary element" to "wrong only where maxwell/thermal group membership diverges" **FIX LANDED 2026-08-26** (Codex+Grok audited round, `tmp/ai_exchange/dr30_stale_thermal_peer.md`): both `compute_superconductor`/`_ts` use the thermal peer's T only when the peer's linked element id equals the current element's, else `gTbulk` — same contract as the existing block-absent branch. Both auditors independently established the firing path is **bulk nodal recovery over aura elements** (`Postprocessor::recover_fields` walks `mMyElementIndices`, whose second pass adds the aura for non-TS blocks, `cl_FEM_Postprocessor.cpp:252-264`) — reachable today on ≥2 ranks, since each kernel expands its own aura; TS recovery (side-local) and `compute_element_data` (owned-only) never trigger it. Anchor refresh: linker silent-keep now comment-documented at `cl_FEM_Calculator.cpp:3016-3037`. Residuals kept open: T not in `mPostprocessorSourceFields`; `gTbulk` on maxwell-only aura cells biases partition-boundary nodal J/Jc when T is far from bulk (complete fix = expand thermal aura to maxwell's); pre-existing `norm(N*q)` vs assembly `dot(Nvec,q)`+clamp mismatch. **GATE EXECUTED AND ROW CLOSED 2026-08-26** (four runs, two decks, serial and 2-rank, cold start, all exit 0; counter probe in both compute functions, since removed). **The nominated gate was misspecified and is struck, not passed:** corc structurally cannot reach the guarded code on either path -- it declares no volume conductor block, so `MaxwellFactory` never constructs the bulk `SuperConductor` postprocessor (`cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp:2318` keys on `s == 0` volume blocks with `have( jc )`), and its thin-shell blocks are excluded from the aura pass (`cl_FEM_Postprocessor.cpp:258`), so the TS peer is always current. No shipped deck does better: the `tapestack3d` family's bulk blocks are `solder` (no `jc`, builds `Conductor`, never touches the thermal peer) and the only bulk-ybco deck, `bigtape`, has no thermal section. A substitute deck was built (`tapestack3d` with blocks 2:8 repointed to a power-law `hts` material -- deliberately unphysical, recipe in the devlog, not checked in). **Result, thermal reads per postprocessing pass:** bulk path serial 1 334 102 vs 2-rank 1 147 784 + 331 034 = 1 478 818, i.e. **+144 716 (10.8 %) redundant reads = the aura, so the audit's reachability claim is CONFIRMED by measurement**; TS path sums to exactly the serial count in both decks (380 160 and 468 512, zero overhead), confirming the thin-shell aura skip. **Stale-element count: 0 in every configuration** -- across 1.48 M reads/pass over four passes on a 78/22 partition, including all 144 716 aura reads, the thermal group contained every element the maxwell group did, so `element_exists()` never failed. The fix is therefore correct, harmless and defensive-in-practice: the path is live, the condition is not. No regression -- corc terminal voltages agree between 1 and 2 ranks to 1e-8 .. 1e-6 relative on all six tapes. Residuals stay open as before (T not in `mPostprocessorSourceFields`; `gTbulk` bias if the groups ever diverge; `norm(N*q)` vs `dot(Nvec,q)`+clamp). **Ruled 2026-08-26: the silent-keep in `link_element_maxwell_thermal` STAYS** and must not be tightened into an assert or an error -- it is the sanctioned fallback for a maxwell element with no thermal counterpart, kept because coupled models added later may need it; latent is not dead. The obligation sits on the CONSUMER, which the postproc element-id comparison discharges. | ~~DR-31~~ | ~~postproc: quadratic edge-field synch — one value per edge vs two dofs per edge. **CONFIRMED A REAL BUG 2026-08-11 (was `unverified`), fix written, not yet run.** The layout check the row asked for was done end to end and the suspicion holds. `DofManager::create_fields` sizes an edge field as `edge_multiplicity() * number_of_edges()` (`cl_FEM_DofManager.cpp:215`), and `IWG_Maxwell` sets that multiplicity to **2** whenever the element order is 2 (`cl_IWG_Maxwell.cpp:63-73`), storing the two dofs of edge `i` at `2i` and `2i+1` — exactly how `Calculator::nedelec_data_quadratic_2d/3d` reads them back. But `Postprocessor::synch_source_field` copied **one** value per entity index (`tData( tCount++ ) = tSource( tIndex )`), and `mAllEdgeIndices`/`mMyEdgeIndices` hold *entity* indices, not dof positions. So for a quadratic field it read slot `i` out of a `2N`-long array: the second dof of every edge was never transferred at all, and the value that *was* transferred belonged to a different edge. `face_h` (multiplicity 2, `number_of_faces()`) has the identical defect. **Not a crash** — the indices stay in bounds, so it is silent wrong data on aura entities, i.e. wrong recovered H/B/J near partition boundaries. **Reachability is not opt-in:** `mElementOrder = mMesh->max_element_order()` (`cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp:87`) and the flag is `mElementOrder == 2` (`:560`), so simply loading a second-order mesh and running on >1 rank hits it; `MaxwellPostprocessor` puts `edge_h`/`face_h` in the source list whenever `face_h` exists (`cl_MaxwellPostprocessor.cpp:99-106,131-138`). **Fix:** recover the stride from the field's own length (`tSource.length() / tNumEntities`) rather than assuming 1, and copy `multiplicity` values per index on both the send and receive side, with an assert that the length divides evenly and that the received count matches. Derived from the field rather than queried from the IWG so the two cannot drift apart. Compiles clean under `-DNDEBUG` and `-DDEBUG` with `-Werror`; **never executed** — and note the serial path is untouched, so only a ≥2-rank run on a second-order mesh exercises it. **Severity raised P2 → P1 on the wrong-answer class; the blocking-1.0 flag is Christian's call and turns on whether order-2 meshes are in scope for the release**~~ | P1 (was P2) | **fix written 2026-08-11, **COMMITTED `0671b29a` (2026-08-11), still unrun**. **Re-verified against the tree 2026-08-13 sweep:** `0671b29a` is an ancestor of HEAD and `git diff 0671b29a -- cl_FEM_Postprocessor.cpp` is EMPTY, so the committed fix is the working-tree state. The stride is recovered from the field itself (`:745-757`, `tMultiplicity = tSource.length() / tNumEntities` with an exact-division assert), and both the send (`:771-782`) and receive (`:803-816`) loops index `tMultiplicity * tIndex + k`. The path is entity-type agnostic, so `edge_h` AND `face_h` are both covered by one code path — the row's "identical defect in face_h" is fixed by construction, not by a second patch. Two residuals worth knowing: the asserts are `BELFEM_ASSERT`, so under NDEBUG a non-dividing length truncates silently via integer division rather than faulting; and no test anywhere under `tests/` mentions `synch_source_field` or `edge_multiplicity`, so the gate really is a run. **Recommend striking with DR-52/64/66 (Christian's call) — fixed, committed, tree-verified; only the run remains.** **2026-08-24: the run was ATTEMPTED and is blocked upstream by DR-102** — no order-2 gmsh mesh loads at all ( `face_key_3d` key collisions abort `Face::compute_orientation` during mesh setup, before this row's synch path can ever execute ); the gate resumes the moment DR-102's fix lands. A side-finding of the attempt: plausibly no gmsh order-2 mesh has ever been loaded end-to-end, so this row's "simply loading a second-order mesh hits it" reachability was code-true but never exercisable in practice. **Update, same night: DR-102 is FIXED ( audited, landed )** — its first wall fell, and the mesh now loads deeper, into **DR-103** ( cut-pipeline TET10 node flagging ), which blocks the gate instead; a cut-free order-2 deck would bypass DR-103, but no in-tree 3D deck is simply-connected. **Final state 2026-08-24: DR-103 also FIXED — and the chain then terminates at a DELIBERATE feature boundary**: `create_hanging_edges_and_facets` ( `cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp:1399` ) hard-errors "Not implemented for higher order" by design, and every in-tree 3D h-φ deck computes cohomologies, so no order-2 deck can reach the Maxwell solver — and therefore this row's committed synch fix **cannot be exercised by any run until higher-order hanging-edge support is implemented**. The fix is correct-by-review, committed, and dormant; the row's "ask" resolves itself: order-2 is not a 1.0 feature ( the framework refuses it loudly ), so this cannot block 1.0 **STRUCK 2026-08-26 BY RULING ( Christian: "we also strike DR-31" ) — a recorded DR-42/49-style exception, and the cleanest case of one yet: the gate is not merely unrun but UNRUNNABLE BY DESIGN.** The fix is written, committed ( `0671b29a` ), tree-verified, and covers `edge_h` and `face_h` through one entity-type-agnostic path. Its gate needs an order-2 mesh on >= 2 ranks, and the 2026-08-24 chain established that no such run can reach this code: DR-102 and DR-103 were both found and FIXED on the way, and the chain then terminates at a DELIBERATE feature boundary — `create_hanging_edges_and_facets` ( `cl_MaxwellFactory.cpp:1399` ) carries an always-active `BELFEM_ERROR( max_element_order() == 1, "Not implemented for higher order" )`, and every in-tree 3D h-phi deck computes cohomologies. | ~~DR-82~~ **[CODE]** | ~~io: **every HDF5 loader passes the FILE datatype as the MEMORY datatype, so a width mismatch between writer and reader silently corrupts memory instead of converting or failing.**~~ Found 2026-08-16 while fixing a macOS build break in `cl_Mesh_BfmFile.cpp` (that break itself was only C++ overload resolution — `uint64_t` is `unsigned long` on linux but `unsigned long long` on macOS, and `HDF5` had no `lluint` overload; fixed by adding one, `cl_HDF5.{hpp,cpp}`). All four loaders do `tDataType = H5Dget_type( tDataSet )` and then hand that same id to `H5Dread` as the memory type (`hdf5_tools.hpp:182-194` scalar, `:353-378` cell, `:511-541` vector, `:719-772` matrix). HDF5 therefore performs NO conversion and copies at the FILE's element size: an 8-byte file read into a 4-byte object overruns it; a 4-byte file read into an 8-byte object leaves the upper half uninitialized. Both silent. `check_datatye_size<T>()` cannot catch this — it compares `sizeof(T)` with the MEMORY type (`hdf5_tools.hpp:73`), always equal; the sole class check (`:722-726`) is a `BELFEM_ASSERT`, gone in release. **Not an OS issue: macOS↔linux files are interchangeable** (the writer pins `H5T_ORDER_LE` at `:114` and HDF5 stores a datatype description — class/size/order/sign — not a C type name, so `NATIVE_ULONG` and `NATIVE_ULLONG` are identical on disk). The exposure is **build-config mismatch**: `index_t` (`uint64_t`/`uint32_t`) and `int_t` (`int64_t`/`int32_t`) are flag-selected, and those are exactly the pointer/index arrays written by `SpMatrix::save` and by `.bfm` meshes. Mitigation ladder: (1) on read, `BELFEM_ERROR` when `H5Tget_size( tDataType ) != sizeof( T )` or the classes differ — always-active tier, no format change; (2) pass `datatype<T>()` as the memory type so HDF5 converts, making mixed-width files readable rather than merely rejected; (3) durable: create datasets with fixed-width file types (`H5T_STD_U64LE`, `H5T_STD_I32LE`, `H5T_IEEE_F64LE`) instead of a native copy — backward compatible, since a native LE copy already IS the fixed-width layout on every LP64 platform | P1 | **fixed 2026-08-16 ( Christian: "fix DR-82 and DR-83" ), GATE PASSED 2026-08-27 — verified by execution.** Scratchpad probe linking the prebuilt io/core/comm archives: ( a ) 8-byte u64 dataset read into a uint32_t scalar arrives value-intact via the datatype<T>() memory-type conversion — the formerly-corrupting case; ( b ) a float dataset read into an integer is refused by the always-active class guard with the intended message. Rungs (1)+(2) verified. **STRUCK 2026-08-27 ( Christian: "OK, strike DR-82" )** — the corruption defect is fixed and gate-verified; the hardening residual is spun off as DR-116, so no residual rides this row. Ladder rungs (1)+(2) landed together in `hdf5_tools.hpp`: a new always-active `check_read_datatype<T>()` refuses a datatype-CLASS mismatch ( what conversion cannot fix — a float dataset read into an integer ), called from all four loaders right after `H5Dget_type`; and all four `H5Dread` calls now pass `datatype<T>()` as the MEMORY type, so HDF5 converts widths itself and a mixed-width file becomes READABLE rather than corrupting. The old class check was a BELFEM_ASSERT ( gone in release, which is where this bites ) — the new one is BELFEM_ERROR. Writers deliberately untouched: their `H5Dwrite` memory type stays the LE-ordered `tDataType`. Rung (3) ( fixed-width file types on create ) NOT done — it is the durable fix and a separate round. Note for that round: the writers' memory type is an LE copy of the native type, which is only correct on little-endian hosts — latent, out of scope, no BE target today | write an SpMatrix or mesh from an INT64-configured build, read it from an INT32 build (or hand-write an 8-byte dataset and load it into a 4-byte scalar); same-width round-trip covered 2026-08-16: io suite passes in full ctest (14/14) with the new memory-type path and class guard in | **candidate** — data corruption is silent, but only across mismatched build configs |

| ~~DR-106~~ **[RUN]** **[P]** | ~~sparse/MUMPS + controller: **a MUMPS −9 soft-fail ( real-workspace exhaustion from dynamic pivoting ) is answered by a TIMESTEP CUT instead of a workspace retry — a misclassified failure that silently pins Δt at the floor.** Found 2026-08-24 in the tape_quench four-run comparison: BDF5/mumps hit −9 41 times, BDF1+gauge 14 times; each soft-fails ( the `soft_fail()` arms in `cl_SolverMUMPS.cpp` ) and the controller treats it as a rejected step, halving Δt — which "works" only because smaller Δt → more diagonal dominance → fewer delayed pivots, so the run crawls at the floor while the actual remedy is more workspace. **The code prescribes its own fix**: the `ICNTL(14): workspace relaxation` comment in `cl_SolverMUMPS.cpp` hardcodes `ICNTL(14) = 30` with the comment "raise it for very ill-conditioned problems ( or add a retry-on-(-9) loop )" — the retry loop is the standard MUMPS pattern ( on −9: double the relaxation, refactorize, only then report failure ), and a deck key for the relaxation would complete it ( two-artifact rule applies when landed ). STRUMPACK is unaffected ( zero failures on the identical deck )~~ | P2 | open ( found 2026-08-24 ) — well-defined small fix round: retry-on-(-9) with doubled `ICNTL(14)` inside the MUMPS wrapper before the soft-fail hand-back, plus an optional `memory relaxation` deck key; red/green reproducer exists ( `build/tape_quench/out_bdf5.txt` config ) **CITATIONS RE-ANCHORED 2026-08-30** after a parallel session ( belfem-fe ) hit this row live and reported the drift: the soft-fail site was cited at `:371-384`, which is now a warning-message loop, and the ICNTL(14) comment at `:99-104`, now `:101-107`. Both are replaced by searchable tokens per CLAUDE.md's own rule, because a line number read from a working copy is already wrong by the time it is committed. Note the shape has ALSO changed: there are now THREE `soft_fail()` arms ( around `:285`, `:675`, `:850` ), not the single site the row described, so a fix must cover all of them. **Independently confirmed while checking this:** `ICNTL(14)` is written INSIDE `mumpstools_solve` ( `mumpstools.f90:404-405`, the subroutine opening at `:241` ), i.e. per solve call rather than once at initialization, so a recycled MUMPS instance cannot carry a stale relaxation into a later solve — that rules the DR-144 instance-lifecycle class OUT for this symptom. **But the `.gt. 0` guard on that write has a separate consequence worth its own look:** when `MemoryRelaxation` is 0 the wrapper does NOT write `ICNTL(14)` at all, so a solve on a RECYCLED instance silently inherits whatever relaxation the previous owner set instead of falling back to MUMPS's own default. Latent today because the C++ side forces 30 everywhere, and NOT the cause of the observed -9s; recorded here rather than filed as a new row, per the extend-don't-branch policy **JURY ROUND 2026-08-30 ( `tmp/ai_exchange/review_mumps_icntl14_ceiling.md`; Codex + Grok, blind, parallel ) — this row's prescribed fix has a LOAD-BEARING implementation trap it did not name.** ( 1 ) **The retry cannot be keyed on a rank-local code.** The Fortran shim exports `INFO`, not `INFOG`: the integer copy-out is `forall( k=1:40 ) aInfo( k ) = tMUMPS%INFO( k )` while only the REAL array is `RINFOG`. MUMPS sets `INFO(1) = -9` on the failing rank and `INFO(1) = -1`, with the failing rank id in `INFO(2)`, on every other rank. `print_soft_fail` is rank-0 only and rank 0 was never the failing rank in the observed runs ( failing ranks 7, 4, 1, 6 ), so **the `-9` arm of `print_soft_fail` is dead code on an 8-rank job** — every box reads "error on rank N" and never names the workspace, which is why the log's `-9` values are readable only from raw MUMPS stdout. A retry written as `if ( mInfo( 0 ) == -9 )` therefore fires on ONE rank and leaves the others outside the collective MUMPS call: deadlock, or a retry of every negative code including `-10` ( singular ). The retry MUST first establish a rank-uniform code, by exporting `INFOG(1:40)` from the shim or by an allreduce of the failing rank's `INFO(1:2)`. Raised independently by BOTH auditors and re-verified against the shim by this session. ( 2 ) **Re-analysis already happens and is not the missing ingredient.** After a soft fail the wrapper sets `mMatrix = nullptr`, forcing JOB 6 on the next attempt, but never raises `MemoryRelaxation` — so every retry in a streak re-analyses and still meets `ICNTL(14) = 30`. Re-analysis and more workspace solve different problems; MUMPS prescribes raising `ICNTL(14)` and repeating the FACTORIZATION ( JOB 2, or JOB 5 ) for `-9`, reserving re-analysis for the analysis-phase errors `-5` / `-7`. A correct retry raises the relaxation and retries JOB 2/5 WITHOUT nulling `mMatrix`. ( 3 ) **The retry must sit inside the wrapper, before `flag_failure()`.** The controller aborts the run after eight consecutive solver failures ( `mSolverFailCount` in `cl_FEM_Controller.cpp`, reset on an accepted step ), so a retry placed above the wrapper still loses a run that needs nine; a warm-restart session on 2026-08-30 died to exactly that cap after 8 fails walking Δt from 0.5 ms down to 0.0039 ms. ( 4 ) **That abort names the wrong failure** — it reports a recoverable factorization-workspace event as "persistently singular or not converging", the third-category miss `doc/coding_philosophy.md` warns about, and it belongs to this row. ( 5 ) **`ICNTL(23)` is a documented FOURTH option** not previously considered here: it is written nowhere in `src/`, and the MUMPS 5.9.0 guide recommends a per-rank working-memory cap in PARALLEL precisely because parallel analysis estimates can be inaccurate. A sizing lever, not a substitute for the retry. ( 6 ) Scope: of the three `soft_fail()` arms, the multi-RHS one is NOT live for this deck family ( `num_rhs_cols == 1` ), so the live magnetic path is the single-RHS arm plus the create arm. ( 7 ) `INFOG(1) = -9` with positive `INFOG(2)` = count of missing entries CONFIRMED against the manual ( MUMPS 5.9.0 error `-9`; largest observed shortfall 2 567 742 entries ≈ 19.6 MiB at 8 B/entry ), upgrading that reading from inference to literature. ( 8 ) **Do NOT read a quiet run as a closed exposure:** a 2026-08-30 `tape_quench_usermat` run on a REFINED mesh ( 227 468 free magnetic dofs against 148 400 on the previous mesh ) took 24 steps with ZERO `-9` events, where the previous mesh produced 24 soft fails on the same deck — different matrix, different pivoting, NOT evidence this row is fixed. Also refuted in that round and recorded so it is not re-derived: there is no Δt threshold ( failures are not monotone in Δt — one session failed at every Δt down to 0.0039 ms while another succeeded at 0.0420 ms ), and "raise the hardcoded 30 to 100-150" is the WEAKEST option ( framework-wide; both auditors ranked it last, behind the in-wrapper retry and behind a deck-only STRUMPACK switch as the zero-rebuild experiment ). **FIXED 2026-08-30 ( joint session with DR-144 R12/R13, `todo/closed/dr106_dr144_joint_session.md` ) — run gates owed.** The retry landed INSIDE the wrapper per constraint (3): shim exports rank-uniform `INFOG(1:40)` ( new `aInfoG` argument, five call sites; create/free deliberately kept on `INFO` — the plan jury found the INFOG-after-failed-JOB=-1 contract unproven and a wrong rollback key would reopen DR-144's slot leak ), shared `escalate_workspace()` ladder in both solve overloads doubles `ICNTL(14)` 30→60→120→240 ( persist-bounded, `free()` restores 30; zero-relaxation hang guard + overshoot clamp from the code jury ), retry job = JOB 5 anchored to the MUMPS user-guide quotes in the todo ( ICNTL(14) is read at BOTH analysis and factorization, so no re-analysis; JOB 2 alone would return the RHS copy as solution through this shim ). `print_soft_fail`/`error_message`/error arms decode `INFOG`; controller abort text reworded at all 4 sites without claiming a wrapper-specific retry. Two blind jury rounds ( plan terra/high+grok/high, code diff BOTH at xhigh ), all auditor-prescribed fixes applied; `-fsyntax-only` clean under Armadillo, Blaze and no-MUMPS defines — REVIEWED NOT VERIFIED, no object built ( tree held by the live tape_quench run ). Owed: `make check` rebuild ( R7 ) and the ONLY real gate, a deck rerun reproducing `-9` ( R8, tape_quench BDF5/mumps ) — the retry has no unit-level trigger. Linked MUMPS verified 5.9.1 vs guide 5.9.0 read, same minor. **STRUCK 2026-08-30 ( Christian's ruling ) — both gates RAN the same afternoon.** Rebuild 14:49–14:50 ( shim + wrapper in one pass ), `check-fast` 15 of 15 suites green incl. `sparse`. The restarted `tape_quench_usermat` run on the new binary hit `-9` TWICE at t = 109.18 ms and the ladder recovered both: the step-791 magnetic solve walked ICNTL(14) 30→60→120→240 ( raw INFOG(2) shortfall 214 634 → 3 421 817 → 8 327 358 — the shortfall GROWS with the relaxation because more pivoting is admitted ) and the step then CONVERGED ( magnetic 5.95e-08, thermal 1.00e-08, 'timestep succeeded' ); the eigen/conditioning probe's own MUMPS instance laddered independently to 240 and its analysis completed ( 6013 ms, COND1 5.55e9 ) — per-instance persistence as designed, that instance is created and freed per call. Zero soft-fail boxes in the entire log; run healthy per Christian. Evidence: `cmake-build-debug/tape_quench_usermat/out.txt` lines 34758–34812 @ 14:51, `Testing/Temporary/LastTest.log` @ 14:50. | ~~DR-144~~ **[RUN]** **[W]** | ~~sparse: **MUMPS instance-lifecycle accounting has seven pre-existing gaps that can desynchronize C++ wrapper state from Fortran pool occupancy, deadlock a mixed-occupancy pool, or report the wrong cause.** ( 1 ) a failed `DMUMPS( JOB = -1 )` leaves its slot occupied — occupy+increment happen before the call and nothing rolls back on error ( `mumpstools.f90` create ); ( 2 ) `MUMPS::initialize` ignores the `tInfo` the shim returns — a positive solver ID with a failing `INFO(1)` passes the `mSolverID > 0` gate ( `cl_SolverMUMPS.cpp` create site ); ( 3 ) `MUMPS::mInitialized` is set only in `solve`, so initialize-then-free-before-any-solve skips `mumpstools_free_solver` ( `free` runs it only under that flag ) and leaks the slot while `Wrapper::free()` clears the retry flag — the next solve creates a SECOND slot; ( 4 ) the matrix-RHS solve overload sets `mInitialized` ONLY in its soft-fail branch ( `cl_SolverMUMPS.cpp:783` ) and never on success ( the vector overload sets it at `:643` ), so an instance used exclusively through successful matrix-RHS solves is skipped by `free()`'s `mInitialized` gate and never receives `JOB = -2` — verified 2026-08-29, no in-tree caller currently solves matrix-RHS-only. ( 5 ) **pool exhaustion in `mumpstools_create_solver` is NON-collective**: the `-1000` branch returns without the `MPI_BARRIER` the success path takes ( `mumpstools.f90:91-92` vs `:103-105` ), so a pool that ever went mixed-occupancy across ranks would deadlock inside Fortran, ahead of every C++ guard DR-143's Fix C added — the concrete shape of the deadlock risk both vendors could rate only medium-high; inherited from the old high-water allocator, NOT introduced by the first-free scan, and not constructible from any current driver because every create/free in the tree is collective; ( 6 ) **`report_unavailable` mislabels a solver failure**: its reason ternary ( `cl_FEM_DofMgr_EigenValues.cpp:1561-1571` ) has arms for NotConverged / Complex / NotPositiveDefinite / Inconsistent and a default that IS the Infeasible message, so an `EigenOutcome::SolverFailed` — including registry exhaustion under soft-fail — is reported to the user as "no legal Krylov subspace fits the basis budget". Wrong cause, and precisely the misdirection class that made DR-143 expensive to diagnose; ( 7 ) unbounded free ID: `mumpstools_free_solver` indexes `gOccupied( aSolverID )` with no bound check, and `mumpstools_set_num_max_solvers` does not reallocate the pool, so a post-allocate raise walks the scan past `size( gOccupied )` and a lower can deallocate while high slots are occupied — unreachable today, the setter has no C++ caller anywhere ( `mumpstools.hpp:28` is a declaration only ). Filed 2026-08-29 from the DR-143 audit rounds ( Codex plan finding 1.4 + Grok open risk; gap 4 from Codex's code-audit adjacent findings; gaps 5-7 from Grok's code-audit leg, citations re-verified against the tree ), deliberately kept OUT of that diff per the no-scope-creep rule. None is live today: MUMPS `JOB = -1` failure is not observed in practice, no path frees between initialize and first solve, and no in-tree path drives the pool to mixed occupancy~~ | P3 | open — fix shape: roll back occupy on `JOB = -1` error in the shim, check `tInfo` beside `mSolverID` at the create site, and either set `mInitialized` in a successful `initialize()` or key the Fortran free on `mSolverID > 0`; needs its own plan round because the `mInitialized` semantics carry the frozen-factorization and soft-fail machinery. Gap 5 wants the barrier moved AHEAD of the occupancy decision, or the exhaustion branch given its own barrier, so both outcomes are collective — cheap, but it changes a collective's shape and belongs in the same round. Gap 6 is a one-line ternary arm and is the highest value per line in the entry: it fixes a message that actively points the next debugging session at the wrong subsystem. Gap 7 needs only a bound check in the free plus honouring the `mumpstools.hpp:28` contract ( or removing the unused setter ). **PLAN WRITTEN AND JURY-AUDITED 2026-08-29** ( `todo/closed/dr144_mumps_lifecycle_plan.md`; Christian scoped it "DR-144 in full, one round" ). Codex returned *revise before implementation*, Grok *approve with the required corrections*; both confirmed the gap inventory and both rejected the STEP shapes. **The seven gaps are now NINE.** G8: a second `initialize()` creates a slot and overwrites `mSolverID` before `Wrapper::initialize()` throws on its already-initialized check, so BOTH slots leak and the first becomes unreachable ( Grok ). G9: `mumpstools_create_solver` passes `aInfo`, an `integer( int_t )` that is 64-bit under `BELFEM_INT64`, as `MPI_BARRIER`'s status argument at `:103`, where a default `integer` is required — `mumpstools_free_solver` gets this right at `:127` — and the value is clobbered at `:107` regardless ( Grok ). **Three of the filed fix shapes were wrong and are corrected in the plan:** the bound check must come AFTER the `allocated` guard, because `size()` on an unallocated allocatable is undefined ( both auditors, independently ); moving the create barrier ahead of the occupancy decision does NOT make exhaustion collective, it relocates the hang into `DMUMPS`, which is itself collective on `MPI_COMM_WORLD` — the honest step is a barrier on each agreeing arm plus a separate allreduce question, logged as O4 ( both auditors ); and keying `free()` on `mSolverID > 0` without zeroing the ID enables a CROSS-WRAPPER DESTROY rather than a benign double free, since a freed-but-remembered ID names a slot the first-free scan has since handed to someone else ( Grok, refuting the row's own proposed shape ). Also refuted: this row's claim that gaps 3 and 4 "need their own plan round because the `mInitialized` semantics carry the frozen-factorization and soft-fail machinery" — `MUMPS::mInitialized` is written at three solve sites and READ AT EXACTLY ONE, `cl_SolverMUMPS.cpp:284`; the frozen scope is carried by `mFrozen.mArmed`, and the soft-fail retry keys on the SEPARATE `Wrapper::mIsInitialized`. The two flags are different members, which is the trap. Gaps 3 and 4 collapse into one write at successful create. **IMPLEMENTED, CODE-AUDITED, AND GATE-PASSED 2026-08-29**, 9 of 11 steps. **`make check` RAN GREEN ( Christian ), discharging R8 and R9.** Confirmed rather than inferred, per the DR-117 lesson: `cmake-build-debug/test/test_sparse` is dated 22:06 against a last source edit at 21:51, all four MUMPS test names are compiled into that binary, and `sparse` is in the `check` dependency list ( `CMakeLists.txt:422` ) — so the new `SolverLifecycle.MUMPSFreesAnInstanceThatNeverSolved` genuinely executed and is not a scratch-link result. **NOT STRIKEABLE: G10 and G11 are live code residue** ( see below ), so the row keeps `[CODE]` — and keeps `[W]` too, because what now remains is exactly two LATENT defects with no in-tree trigger, which is what `[W]` means. The master recovers via `link_matrix()` on the next `run()`; a worker does not. The block's own comment ( "`link_matrix()` only ever runs on the master" ) is also FALSE — `compute_matrices()` and `run_arpack()` both call it unguarded. Grok, ~70% live. Fix shape: assign unconditionally with a `BELFEM_ERROR( mK != nullptr, ... )`, same cost as the null test. ( G11 ) the `mSolverID <= 0` bail in BOTH `MUMPS::solve` overloads returns silently even when soft-fail is OFF, so a caller that solves without a successful `initialize()` gets `x` unchanged, `failed() == true` and no abort in debug or release; its comment claims it covers only the soft-failed-create case but the code never tests `soft_fail()`. Grok, ~60% reachable. Fix shape: `if( ! this->soft_fail() ) BELFEM_ERROR( ... ) ; else { flag_failure() ; return ; }`. **Both deliberately NOT fixed in this round's diff** — each sits inside a DIFFERENT session's uncommitted work, and the rule is not to edit over that; they are this row's residue and belong to whoever lands that WIP. Third standing debt, already carried in the shim comment: no `JOB = -2` after a failed `JOB = -1`, MUMPS contract unread. Fourth: the pool is now hard-capped at 8 with the setter deleted ( O1 resolved to (b) ), so a multi-field deck each holding a diagnostic instance has no lever left. **G12 ADDED AND FIXED 2026-08-29, raised by Christian from reading a footer:** `MUMPS ADD COND2` printed `1.00e0` on most timesteps and that is not a measurement. Traced to the shipped MUMPS source rather than the user guide — `DMUMPS_SOL_OMEGA` ( `dsol_aux.F:886-899` ) initializes `OMEGA(2)` to zero and writes it only for rows it puts in the second category, and `DMUMPS_SOL_LCOND` ( `:959-1033` ) reads that same partition, initializes BOTH condition numbers to 1.0, and on an empty category executes `IF (.NOT.LCOND2) GOTO 170`, returning past the whole COND2 estimator AND past `ERX = ERX + OMEGA(2)*COND(2)`. The printed 1.0 is therefore an untouched initializer for a term that does not exist. Fixed by adding `Wrapper::get_omega2()` / `MUMPS::get_omega2()` ( `mRInfoG( 7 )` = RINFOG(8) ), capturing it into a fourth slot of the controller's conditioning vectors, and OMITTING the row when omega2 is exactly zero. Keyed on omega2 rather than on `COND2 == 1.0` because the sentinel is indistinguishable from a genuine estimate near one; omitted rather than printed `n/a` because the two claims differ — `n/a` means asked and unavailable, this means no such quantity exists for the solve — and the uncaptured case still prints `n/a` unchanged. NOT suppressed unconditionally: COND2 is real in a minority of steps ( 3 of 14 and 5 of 41 in the tapestack3d logs, 5.4e2 to 7.9e3 ), and those are exactly the steps where part of the residual went through the padded denominator. COND1 has the identical skip structure and deliberately did NOT get the guard — omega1 is nonzero in every observed step, so it could not be exercised, and that asymmetry is logged as O6 rather than papered over. **Code-audit round on G12 ( both vendors ) CORRECTED THE JUSTIFICATION, not the gate.** Both found that `omega2 == 0` does NOT prove the second row category was empty: `DMUMPS_SOL_OMEGA` assigns `IW( i, 1 ) = 2` OUTSIDE the `IF ( TAU .GT. ZERO )` guard that updates omega2 ( `dsol_aux.F:891-900` ), so a row with `TAU == 0` or an exactly vanishing residual joins the category, sets `LCOND2`, lets the Hager estimator RUN, and still leaves omega2 at zero. Empty category implies omega2 == 0; the converse does not hold, and MUMPS documents only the forward direction ( `dini_defaults.F:1062-1065` ). My comments had asserted the biconditional and were wrong. **Codex rejected the patch on it, Grok approved the code and rejected only the comments — Grok's reading was adopted**: at omega2 == 0 the term is zero either way, so a suppressed COND2 cannot move the error bound it exists to explain, and the true discriminator ( `IW` ) is internal to MUMPS and recoverable from neither omega2 nor COND2. **The residual case is accepted, not overlooked**: in that rare configuration a genuinely estimated COND2 is suppressed, and the helper's comment now says so in as many words. Fixed in the same pass: all three overstated comments rewritten to the forward-error-term framing, the base-class contract restated without MUMPS internals, the stale slot-ownership comment in `compute_conditioning` extended to slot 3, and the helper renamed `add_cond2_is_real` -> `cond2_row_wanted` ( it returns TRUE for NaN, so the old name contradicted its own contract ). Syntax-clean; `make check` not re-run since, and no test covers footer formatting or calls `get_omega2` **R12/R13 ( G10/G11 ) LANDED 2026-08-30 in the DR-106 joint session — 11 of 11 steps now done, rebuild gate owed.** G10: unconditional `mK = mParent->jacobian()` recapture + `BELFEM_ERROR` null check in `run_parpack`, false master-only comment corrected ( `link_matrix()` runs unguarded at :370/:456 too ). G11: both `mSolverID <= 0` bails now `BELFEM_ERROR` unless `soft_fail()` is armed, else-shape. Audited in the joint round's xhigh code jury; reviewed-not-verified until the joint session's R7 rebuild runs. **STRUCK 2026-08-30 ( Christian's ruling ) — nothing remains on the row.** All 11 steps landed once R12 ( G10 unconditional `mK` recapture ) and R13 ( G11 loud bail ) rode the DR-106 joint session; the lifecycle gates ( init-then-free unit test, pool-recycle test ) had run green 2026-08-29, and the 2026-08-30 rebuild compiled R12/R13 into the `check-fast`-green binary ( 15 of 15 suites incl. `sparse` ). G10/G11 have no reachable in-tree trigger, so the green suite is the maximum evidence this row can produce — struck is not verified, and there is no further gate to name. | ~~DR-138~~ | ~~circuit: **inductor current loss through `ElectricalCircuit::shift_back()` — fixed and unit-verified; one coupled gate left.** The walk called `Inductor::shift_back()` ( reverting `mH`/`mI` ) and then `compute_current()` against the UN-reverted companions `mRL`/`miLh` left by the rejected `shift()`, so the reconstructed current was wrong and the retry's `shift()` pushed that wrong value into the history register. Inductor-specific: capacitor history is node voltages, which `mPrevX` does restore, whereas inductor current is not an unknown-current dof and `update_components()` cannot restore it. Measured at 7.4e-3 A on a 1 A carrier — the magnitude the one-step-ahead-companion mechanism predicts ( ≈ amplitude·2π·dt/T ), far above truncation and far below the O(1) of a counter bug. **The defect was live on BOTH the demo reject path AND the coupled `reset_timestep()` → next `initialize_*` `shift()` sequence — that second path IS the recipe for the owed A/B**; `initialize_timestep` and `initialize_magnetic` still do `set_timestep` then `shift` ( `cl_FEM_Controller.cpp:247-248`, `:381-382` )~~ | P3 | **STRUCK 2026-08-30 ( Christian's DR-42/DR-49 single-run exception ).** The fix landed 2026-08-29 and its UNIT gate RAN: `RLCRingAcrossRejectedStep` red pre-fix at 7.4e-3 A on a 1 A carrier, green post-fix under 1e-3 in the full suite, exactly as pre-registered. `Inductor::shift_back` reverts the registers and recomputes the companions from the reverted state via a private `update_companions()` shared with `shift()`; `Capacitor` gets the same. **The vendor split was resolved BY THE TREE and the losing variant must not be re-proposed:** Codex approved a snapshot-at-shift, Grok refuted it — every live path calls `set_timestep` BEFORE `shift`, which clobbers `mRL` with the BDF1 seed, so the snapshot would restore the seed and leave ~2e-3 A, still red. **THE COUPLED REJECT-RETRY A/B IS WAIVED WITH THIS ROW. It was never executed, so the coupled level remains REVIEWED, NOT VERIFIED.** What the 2026-08-30 session added before the waiver, recorded because it is not in the unit gate: ( 1 ) the owed A/B was found MIS-SCOPED — the controller's reject/retry sequence on the circuit object is call-for-call what `RLCRingAcrossRejectedStep` already runs ( reject = `reset_timestep()` -> `mCircuit->shift_back()`; retry = `set_timestep` -> `shift` -> `compute_MNA_matrix` ), so an A/B would re-measure green arithmetic; what the coupled path genuinely adds is the **FEM leg**. ( 2 ) That leg carries DR-138's EXACT shape — `ElectricalCircuit::shift_back()` reverts `FEMTwoTerminals`' registers then calls `compute_current()`, which reads the **un-reverted** `mIn` — and a scratchpad probe EXECUTED it: the leg is safe **entirely because `set_current_and_voltage` is accept-only** ( sole call chain `Controller::compute_circuit_current()` <- `Controller::finalize()` ). `shift_back()` protects `mIn` not at all: poisoning it with a diverged iterate survives the rollback into the retry's MNA stamp and moves the terminal current 91x more than the faithful case. **Standing hazard: any future path that writes `mIn` on a non-accepted attempt re-creates DR-138 on the FEM leg silently.** ( 3 ) The coupled `examples/circuit` deck rejects timestep 17 naturally and the reject-retry path executed SIX times without abort, with DR-118's accepted-state-only output contract holding coupled ( rejected attempt wrote no row; retry carried the halved dt exactly ). No trajectory A/B was possible: no rejection-free reference twin exists for that deck, which rejects at multiple tolerances and step sizes for reasons left undiagnosed. **Attribution trap recorded so it is not mis-read later: the 2026-08-27 `hphirun` abort at this same rejection is DR-115's rank guard ( committed 2.6 h AFTER that log was written ), NOT this row's defect.** The FEM-leg probe is scratchpad-only and never entered the tree; landing it as a permanent `tests/circuit` case is the obvious residue and is deliberately NOT owed by this row.
